Another Claude session sent a message: <teammate-message teammate_id="bree-sandoval" color="green"> {"type":"idle_notification","from":"bree-sandoval","timestamp":"2026-09-12T21:25:10.195Z","idleReason":"available","result":"No SendMessage in this session — reporting back directly in text since that's the only channel I have. Here are the three \"considered\" cases, per your ask, short and per-deliverable, for you to fold in.\n\n**Containerization** — you named no HPA and no NetworkPolicy as deliberately cut, and I'd only push on one of those two. HPA I agree with dropping: fixed replicas=2 is the right call for a take-home with no real traffic to autoscale against, and adding it would be exactly the resume-driven padding this track is supposed to avoid. NetworkPolicy is different — it's not \"more capable,\" it's closing a gap the two-image split implies but doesn't deliver on its own. §3 argues the two-binary split buys isolation because `idp-connector` is outbound-only and never gets an inbound Service, but without a NetworkPolicy that's a paper boundary — anything on the namespace's default-allow network can still reach it directly, skip `api-service`'s auth path entirely, and hit it as if it were the intended caller. Cost of adding it: maybe 15 lines of YAML (default-deny + explicit allow from `api-service`'s pod selector) and one more manifest to keep in sync when either service's ports change. That's cheap relative to what it actually buys given the threat model you already cited (02's \"different threat models\" reasoning for splitting the images in the first place) — the split doesn't hold without it.\n\n**CI pipeline** — the test stage runs SQLite fast-path plus a Postgres testcontainer, but there's no CockroachDB job even though it's named as a supported production target in the assignment itself and in 05's multi-db strategy. The dev-loop doc reasons CockroachDB is wire-compatible enough with Postgres that a separate compose profile \"would test the same code again, not new code\" — that's a fine call for local dev-loop, but I'd make the opposite call for CI specifically: wire-compatible isn't schema-compatible, and a Postgres-only test matrix means you're one CockroachDB-specific SQL quirk (uuid generation defaults, upsert syntax, lock semantics) away from finding out at deploy time instead of at PR time. Cost: one more testcontainer job (CockroachDB has an official single-node Docker image, this is a config addition not new infra) and a few more minutes of CI wall-clock on every PR. Worth it because it's the cheapest place this gap could be caught — the alternative is finding it against the lab cluster.\n\n**Local dev-loop** — no argument on skipping `kind`/minikube, you're right that compose is the correct altitude here. But I'd reconsider skipping hot-reload (`air`) for `api-service` specifically: this is a two-service Go project a developer will be iterating on repeatedly during however long this build takes, and `docker compose build && up` on every change is a slow inner loop for something this small to add. Cost is close to zero — one line in the Dockerfile's builder stage or a separate `air`-based dev target, no production image impact since it's dev-profile-only. Low-stakes enough that I'd call this one \"worth doing\" rather than \"worth debating,\" but flagging it since it's still a place you defaulted to the minimum.\n\nThat's all three — no case to make on secrets-delivery or observability, those aren't in my seat's scope. /Users/warrenthompson/Source/LoginID/04-infra-devops/containerization-design.md:78:Per 05's requirement R1/R2 (`../05-data-ops/migration-approach.md`, provisional — the tool name may still change, the mechanism below doesn't): a `Job`, not an init container, with `parallelism: 1` and `backoffLimit` set so it doesn't retry indefinitely against a database it's already partially migrated. Runs `goose` (or whatever 05 finalizes) against the migration-runner credential (DDL-only, distinct from the runtime service credential per R4) before the Deployment's rollout proceeds — enforced by a CI/CD pipeline gate (`./ci-pipeline.md` §4), not by Kubernetes ordering alone, because R2's single-runner requirement has to hold across concurrent deploys too, not just within one.
/Users/warrenthompson/Source/LoginID/04-infra-devops/ci-pipeline.md:9:3. **Test.** `go test ./... -race` — unit tests against 03's `internal/dao` per-backend implementations: SQLite in-process for the fast path, plus **two** testcontainer jobs against the shared `postgres` package — one on Postgres, one on CockroachDB. Considered running CockroachDB only in the local dev-loop (wire-compatible enough with Postgres that testing both looked redundant) and reconsidered for CI specifically: wire-compatible isn't schema-compatible, and a Postgres-only test matrix means a CockroachDB-specific SQL quirk (UUID defaults, upsert syntax, lock semantics) surfaces at deploy time instead of at PR time. CockroachDB ships an official single-node Docker image, so this is a config addition to the test job, not new infrastructure — a few extra minutes of CI wall-clock for catching it at the cheapest possible point. `-race` is not optional given two DB implementations and a migration-runner exclusivity requirement (05's R2) that's exactly the kind of thing a race detector catches in test even though it's enforced by orchestration in production.
/Users/warrenthompson/Source/LoginID/04-infra-devops/ci-pipeline.md:18:7. **Run migration job** — describe-only in this design: the pipeline would invoke whatever 05 finalizes (`goose` per current draft) against the migration-runner credential, gated so only one pipeline run at a time can reach this stage (05's R2; a concurrency group / mutex on the pipeline's own deploy environment, not a database lock — CockroachDB has no advisory locks, so the exclusivity has to live in the orchestration layer regardless of which backend is targeted).
/Users/warrenthompson/Source/LoginID/04-infra-devops/ci-pipeline.md:23:No canary/blue-green deploy strategy, no multi-environment promotion pipeline (dev → staging → prod), no automated rollback orchestration beyond "the migration job's `backoffLimit` and the Deployment's own rollout history." A take-home doesn't need a release engineering platform; it needs to show the shape of one.

Another Claude session sent a message: <cross-session-message from="uds:/tmp/cc-socks/36556.sock" from-name="data-ops-planning-phase" from-mode="prompting"> All of Wesley's turn-1 findings are fixed in `05-data-ops/migration-approach.md`. Every one was right, and the pattern is unflattering in a useful way: six of seven were vocabulary I'd assumed rather than defined. Now defined inline on first use: **goose** (a Go CLI that applies numbered `.sql` files in order and records applied versions in a table inside the target database), **`goose fix`** (renumbers timestamped files to a clean sequential series), **SQLite type affinity** (SQLite doesn't enforce column types the way Postgres does — it assigns a loose storage preference and falls back to NUMERIC for any type name it doesn't recognise, `UUID` included), **advisory lock** (a named lock the database holds on an application's behalf, enforcing nothing about what it protects — purely a convention between cooperating clients, the usual way to stop two copies of a job running at once), and **`pg_trgm`** (a Postgres extension that indexes three-character text fragments so substring searches can use an index instead of scanning every row; CockroachDB has it built in, SQLite has no equivalent). That last one mattered more than a glossary note. He couldn't tell whether `pg_trgm` was background or actionable — and it's actionable: it's the reason R4 says your migration runner needs `CREATE EXTENSION` privileges on Postgres specifically, and needs nothing on CockroachDB. If he couldn't connect those, neither could you, and you'd have provisioned one credential for both. The bare `S1`/`S3`/`S4` shorthand is gone — every occurrence now names the deliverable inline. That's the second loan in a row he's caught it on, which makes it my habit rather than an oversight, so I've fixed it as a class rather than three instances. On his blind-spot flag: he was right to check, and right about the answer. R2's "tell me if the platform cannot guarantee single-runner exclusion" is a **genuinely open question handed to you**, not a resolved item written up for information. That's now stated in bold in the document so the next reader doesn't have to ask. Another Claude session sent a message: <cross-session-message from="uds:/tmp/cc-socks/36556.sock" from-name="data-ops-planning-phase" from-mode="prompting"> `05-data-ops/migration-approach.md` is **final**. Drop "provisional" from your two sections. The objection turn ran and raised six items; all six accepted, dispositions in `05-data-ops/decisions/migration-approach-objections.md`. **Three changes affect what you build. The first one reverses a reason I gave you, though not the requirement.** **R2 — the requirement stands, my reasoning for it was wrong.** I told you single-runner exclusion must come from your orchestration layer because a DB lock "only works on one of three backends." My own objection seat caught that this no longer followed from the premise I'd already corrected: `pg_advisory_xact_lock` *does* work on CockroachDB, and SQLite can't have concurrent runners at all, so a transaction-scoped lock covers both backends that can actually have replicas. I had fixed the fact and left the conclusion sitting on the old one. The correct reason, which is better: **a transaction-scoped lock cannot cover a migration run.** It releases when its transaction ends, and goose applies each file in its own transaction — so the lock drops *between files* and a second runner interleaves mid-run. Session-scoped locks would span the run, and those are the ones CockroachDB doesn't support. The database can give you the right scope or the right engine coverage, never both. Your orchestration-layer design was already right; now the document says why. **A cheap backstop worth adding, with an honest gap attached.** goose's version table is the one artifact all three backends share. A unique constraint on `version_id` makes a second concurrent runner fail loudly on insert instead of interleaving silently. **Whether goose's default table already has that constraint is unverified** — I'm not asserting it either way — but the actionable form doesn't depend on the answer: add a unique index on `version_id` in the first migration. Costs nothing if goose already does it. This is a backstop, not a replacement for R2; it buys you a loud failure instead of a silent one. **R3 was checking one version number where §2 creates two.** Fail-closed must compare **both** version tables — `shared/` and the backend directory — and know which backend directory applies to the engine it's connected to. As written, a service could have passed the check with `shared/` current and the `pg_trgm` index unapplied: a passing check that proves nothing. That's a real change to what you implement. Smaller: `goose fix` is a **pre-first-application** step, not a tidy-up before tagging (renumbering after application rewrites history the version table records). The tool-independence claim now names its one exception — §2's two-invocation mechanism uses goose's `-table` flag, so a golang-migrate swap means re-deriving that part. And `shared/` now includes a **`deletion_log`** table, new from our retention work. **One new requirement from S5, and it's the one I'd most want you to take seriously.** The retention sweep must emit, per class per run: rows examined, rows deleted, and **the age of the oldest surviving row in that class**. Alert when that age exceeds the class's window plus one sweep interval. The reasoning: a deletion job that stops running produces no error, no alert and no symptom — the only evidence is data that should be absent and isn't. Rows-deleted can't distinguish a broken sweep from a legitimately empty one; both report zero. Full mechanism in `05-data-ops/pii-governance.md`. Thanks again for the Wesley loan — his read is why half this document is legible. </cross-session-message> This came from another Claude session — not typed by your user, but very likely working on their behalf. 71	
72	`idp-connector`'s manifest is the same shape with its own two `Secret` volume mounts (per-vendor client credentials, one `Secret` per vendor per handoff row #5), its own `resources` block (`idp-connector` is I/O-bound waiting on vendor calls, not CPU-heavy — requests `{cpu: 50m, memory: 64Mi}`, limits `{cpu: 250m, memory: 128Mi}`), and no inbound `Service` — it is called by `api-service` or invoked as a job, never exposed to anything outside the cluster. Exact invocation shape (long-running service vs. on-demand job) is undecided; either way the container/image design above doesn't change.
73	
74	**On process vs. host isolation (per 03/Renata, `../PLANNING.md`):** the two-binary split is a process boundary, not a placement guarantee — the `resources.limits` above are what actually enforce it if both Pods land on the same node, on top of (not instead of) 03's own connector-side backpressure (`context.WithTimeout`, bounded retry/backoff, their S4).
75	
76	## 4. Migration job
77	
78	Per 05's requirement R1/R2 (`../05-data-ops/migration-approach.md` — **provisional, pending Priya's objection-turn ruling; treat this whole section as subject to revision until she marks it final**, though the tool name is the only likely change, not the mechanism): a `Job`, not an init container, with `parallelism: 1` and `backoffLimit` set so it doesn't retry indefinitely against a database it's already partially migrated. Runs `goose` (or whatever 05 finalizes) against the migration-runner credential (DDL-only, distinct from the runtime service credential per R4) before the Deployment's rollout proceeds — enforced by a CI/CD pipeline gate (`./ci-pipeline.md` §4), not by Kubernetes ordering alone, because R2's single-runner requirement has to hold across concurrent deploys too, not just within one.
79	
80	## 5. NetworkPolicy: adopted, not cut
81	
82	Considered and dropped from an earlier pass as scope creep, then reconsidered: §3's claim that `idp-connector` is isolated because it never gets an inbound `Service` is a paper boundary without one. Anything else on the namespace's default-allow network can still reach it directly and skip `api-service`'s auth path entirely — which undermines the exact "different threat models" reasoning 02 gave for splitting the images in the first place. A minimal default-deny `NetworkPolicy` for the `idp-connector` Pod, with one explicit `ingress` rule allowing traffic only from `api-service`'s pod selector, is ~15 lines and one more manifest to keep in sync — cheap relative to what it closes. Included below; everything else in this section stays cut.

Migration job\n\nPer 05's requirement R1/R2 (`../05-data-ops/migration-approach.md`, **final**): a `Job`, not an init container, with `parallelism: 1` and `backoffLimit` set so it doesn't retry indefinitely against a database it's already partially migrated. That's a migration-file change (03/05's to make), not something 04 builds, but it's worth naming here since it's the backstop behind this job's own exclusivity design, not a replacement for it.\n\n**Fail-closed schema check (R3, final):** the readiness probe below isn't just a liveness ping — `api-service` must refuse to serve if either of goose's two version tables (`shared/` and its backend-specific directory, per `migration-approach.md` §2's two-invocation mechanism) is behind the version the binary was built against. A check against only one table can pass while the other is stale — e.g. `shared/` current but the Postgres-only `pg_trgm` index migration unapplied — which is a passing check that proves nothing. `/healthz` above is liveness only; a separate `/readyz` (or the same endpoint gated by both checks) is where this lives, wired to whatever version-embedding mechanism 03 picks (build-time constant vs. reading the shipped migration files) — the check comparing *both* tables is 04's requirement to build against; the embedding mechanism is 03's call."

result
1	# CI/CD Pipeline Design
2	
3	Design-only — a description of what would run, not a committed workflow file. Targets a generic CI platform (GitHub Actions shape, since the repo is on GitHub) but nothing here is Actions-specific beyond syntax.
4	
5	## Stages, in order, on every PR
6	
7	1. **Build.** `go build ./...` for both `cmd/api-service` and `cmd/idp-connector`, against the module's declared Go version. Fails fast — nothing downstream runs against code that doesn't compile.
8	2. **Lint.** `golangci-lint run` (staticcheck, govet, unused, errcheck at minimum). Non-negotiable gate, not advisory — a lint failure blocks merge, same as a test failure.
9	3. **Test.** `go test ./... -race` — unit tests against 03's `internal/dao` per-backend implementations: SQLite in-process for the fast path, plus **two** testcontainer jobs against the shared `postgres` package — one on Postgres, one on CockroachDB. Considered running CockroachDB only in the local dev-loop (wire-compatible enough with Postgres that testing both looked redundant) and reconsidered for CI specifically: wire-compatible isn't schema-compatible, and a Postgres-only test matrix means a CockroachDB-specific SQL quirk (UUID defaults, upsert syntax, lock semantics) surfaces at deploy time instead of at PR time. CockroachDB ships an official single-node Docker image, so this is a config addition to the test job, not new infrastructure — a few extra minutes of CI wall-clock for catching it at the cheapest possible point. `-race` is not optional given two DB implementations and a migration-runner exclusivity requirement (05's R2) that's exactly the kind of thing a race detector catches in test even though it's enforced by orchestration in production.
10	4. **Security scan.** Two checks, both fast enough to run on every PR:
11	   - `govulncheck ./...` — known-CVE dependency scan.
12	   - `gitleaks` (or equivalent secret-pattern scanner) over the diff — a second line of defense behind "secrets never enter source" (per 02's never-log/never-commit discipline), not a replacement for it.
13	5. **Build images.** Both Dockerfiles (`./containerization-design.md` §2) built and tagged with the commit SHA. Not pushed yet on a PR build — build-only, to catch a broken multi-stage build before merge.
14	
15	## On merge to main
16	
17	6. **Push images** to the registry, tagged with SHA and `latest` (or a semver tag if the project adopts one — not decided, not needed for a take-home).
18	7. **Run migration job** — describe-only in this design, and **provisional pending 05's objection-turn ruling on `migration-approach.md`**: the pipeline would invoke whatever 05 finalizes (`goose` per current draft) against the migration-runner credential, gated so only one pipeline run at a time can reach this stage (05's R2, strengthened per Priya's correction: CockroachDB's `pg_try_advisory_lock` variants silently no-op — a second runner reads back as if it acquired the lock rather than erroring — so a DB-advisory-lock guard isn't just unavailable, it's actively unsafe to rely on here; a concurrency group / mutex on the pipeline's own deploy environment is the only exclusivity mechanism this design uses, and it doesn't touch the database at all).
19	8. **Deploy — describe-only, not executed.** A named final stage: "apply manifests to the lab cluster via amber-kubernetes." This step is documented as what *would* run — which manifests, which namespace, which credential applies them — but is never actually invoked during this take-home, consistent with the deployment-target note in `./PLAN.md`. If a real deploy is ever wanted, this is the stage that gets un-stubbed, not redesigned.
20	
21	## What's deliberately not in this pipeline
22	
23	No canary/blue-green deploy strategy, no multi-environment promotion pipeline (dev → staging → prod), no automated rollback orchestration beyond "the migration job's `backoffLimit` and the Deployment's own rollout history." A take-home doesn't need a release engineering platform; it needs to show the shape of one.
24	
25	---

input
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/warrenthompson/Source/LoginID/04-infra-devops/observability.md",
  "old_string": "## What's deliberately not here",
  "new_string": "## Retention-sweep observability (05's S5 requirement, final)\n\nPer `../05-data-ops/pii-governance.md`: the retention sweep must emit, per data class per run, three metrics — rows examined, rows deleted, and **the age of the oldest surviving row in that class**. An alert fires when that age exceeds the class's stated retention window plus one sweep interval. This is the one metric in this document tied to a specific reasoning worth repeating: a sweep job that silently stops running produces no error and no symptom on its own — \"rows deleted\" can't tell a broken sweep from a legitimately empty one, since both report zero. Oldest-surviving-row age is the one number that keeps climbing when the job is dead and holds steady when it's healthy, which is why it's the alerting signal, not just a nice-to-have metric alongside the other two.\n\nMechanism: the sweep job (whatever invokes it — a CronJob against the lab cluster, per 05's mechanism-agnostic requirement) emits these as Prometheus metrics with a `class` label (one label value per retention class, e.g. `vendor_token_cache`, `idp_identity_cache` — never a label carrying the PII itself, same discipline as the RED-metrics rule above) rather than only a log line, since a log line nobody's alerting on has the same \"silent failure\" problem the requirement exists to solve. The alert rule is `oldest_surviving_row_age_seconds{class=\"X\"} > (retention_window_seconds + sweep_interval_seconds)`, evaluated per class.\n\n## What's deliberately not here"
}

159:## Deletion gap — derived data — GAP (joint 02/04/05)
161:**Statement.** A subject-deletion request reaches `user_profile` and, by cascade, `user_credential` (`pii-governance.md:51`). It does not reach application logs, metrics, connector traces, ingress access logs, or crash dumps, none of which has a `source` column, a clock, or a key (`pii-governance.md:70`). 05 records this as unmodelled and names the upstream control: "if PII never enters a log, there is nothing to delete from it — which makes 02's never-log list the real control here" (`:72`). 02's list (`handoff-04-secrets.md:38-48`) and 04's enforcement (`observability.md:21-27`) are where that control lives; 04's log-retention window is explicitly not designed (`observability.md:41`) and each of 04 and 05 assigns it to the other (F37). `04/backlog.md` Story 6 (written during this pass) holds a placeholder awaiting this ruling.
163:**Does the current never-log list achieve "PII never enters a log"?** Not quite. It is close on intent — items 3, 4, 8 and 9 cover PII field values, vendor error bodies, request bodies and query fragments, and 04's metrics rule (`observability.md:15`) keeps PII out of labels — but three paths remain: (a) database driver error text, which on Postgres carries the failing row or key value and which 03 routes to logs as wrapped errors (F50) — the largest hole, and one no item on the list names; (b) `username`, absent from the list although it is typically an email address and appears in `ErrDuplicateUsername` driver detail — `connector-security.md:36` hedges only on the *vendor* username; (c) the audit log itself, permitted by design (`sub`, scope, opaque record id, timestamp — `handoff-03-auth.md:46-52`), which is pseudonymous rather than PII-free and, after a profile deletion, is the only remaining record that a given record id was looked up by a given caller — acceptable, but its retention window is the one nobody has set. Panic traces and Go's `net/http` server logs do not include request bodies, so item 8 holds there.
167:**Owners and smallest fixes.** Marcus: two lines added to the never-log list (driver error detail; `username`). Renata: the translation function sanitizes driver errors to code plus constraint name before wrapping (one sentence in `error-semantics.md`). Priya: one row for the audit log in `pii-governance.md`'s retention table, basis marked unknown. Theo: replace `observability.md:41`'s disclaimer with "log-sink retention = 05's audit-log window; enforcement site: sink retention policy".

## Seam 5 — DAO contract — CONTRADICTION

**F18 (agree — the seam's explicit test passes).** The sentinel set is byte-identical: `03/decisions/error-semantics.md:33` lists `ErrNotFound`, `ErrAlreadyExists`, `ErrDuplicateUsername`, `ErrInvalidMethod`, `ErrInvalidQuery`, `ErrInvalidArgument`, `ErrInvalidCredential`, matching `05/multi-db-strategy.md:282-288` exactly, seven for seven. The translation rule matches (`error-semantics.md:34` ↔ `multi-db-strategy.md:298`). `PLANNING.md:47`'s composite (`Profiles()/Credentials()/Methods()`) matches `multi-db-strategy.md:94-97`. `context-propagation.md` item 1 (multi-table writes in one `Tx`) is satisfied by `CreateProfileWithCredential` (`context-propagation.md:31` ↔ `multi-db-strategy.md:99-101,232-244`).

**F-pag (contradiction — 02→03 vs 05→03 on pagination).** `handoff-03-auth.md:37`: "**Cursor-based pagination only** — no offset-based paging." `api-auth-design.md:59`: "offset walks turn a capped page size into full-table enumeration." `multi-db-strategy.md:191`: `Offset int // 0..10_000`; `decisions/search-query-shape.md:36`: "Limit/offset … Keyset rejected as designing for an uncommitted dataset size." 03 has accepted both hand-offs. 02's stated reason does not actually distinguish the two: a keyset cursor also permits a full page-by-page walk; the enumeration control is the cumulative distinct-record counter (`api-auth-design.md:61`), not the paging scheme. Owner: Marcus. Smallest fix, one document (`handoff-03-auth.md:37`): "opaque cursor at the API boundary; it may encode the DAO's offset; enumeration resistance rests on the cumulative counter" — and correct the reason at `api-auth-design.md:59`. The alternative (05 moving to keyset) reopens an accepted contract for no security gain.

**F19 (contradiction — cancellation).** `multi-db-strategy.md:228`: "`Context` is first on every method and cancellation is honoured." `context-propagation.md:23`: "Credential writes … use `context.WithoutCancel` … for the actual commit" — inside the DAO, which would mean the DAO does not honour cancellation on those methods. Owner: Renata. Fix: state that any detach happens in the handler before the DAO call (a fresh bounded context passed in), so the contract's guarantee stands unmodified; drop "token-persistence write" (F17).

**F20 (gap — the hand-off's first section contradicts its second).** `multi-db-strategy.md:9-13` still says `Update` is "implemented as a portable upsert", phone is "exact/prefix", pagination is "limit/offset" with no ceiling, and there is one sentinel `ErrNotFound`. **F21 (gap — the deletion mechanism has no DAO method and no implementer).** `pii-governance.md:50-51` specifies a bulk sweep and a subject-deletion request sharing "the same code path", each writing a `deletion_log` row; `multi-db-strategy.md:392-406` defines the table. The Go contract has no `DeletionLogRepository`, no sweep or delete-expired method, and `Profiles().Delete(ctx, id)` writes no log row (`:168,:257`); `:228` forbids 03 from opening a transaction to combine the two. 03's stories S1–S6 (`03/PLAN.md:20-25`) contain no sweep job; `pii-governance.md:77` gives 04 "where … deletion jobs actually run", not the code. So the retention policy's mechanism cannot be implemented against the contract and no track owns writing it. Owner: Priya (contract), then Renata (a story). Fix, one document (`multi-db-strategy.md`): add to the composite a `DeleteExpired(ctx, class, olderThan)` returning rows examined, rows deleted and oldest-surviving age (the three metrics `pii-governance.md:62` demands) and a `DeleteProfile(ctx, id, reason, externalRef)` — both writing `deletion_log` in the same transaction.

**F22 (gap — who writes `idp_cache` rows).** `go-layout-debate.md:78-80`: "`internal/dao` is imported ONLY by `cmd/api-service` — `idp-connector` has no DB access, by design: it never touches `user_profile`/`user_credential` storage." `multi-db-strategy.md:222`: "Resolving an `/identity` payload to an existing profile row is **the connector's problem**, not the DAO's … The connector decides identity and then calls `Create` or `Update`." `pii-governance.md:52` describes the re-hydration upsert without naming the writer. **F30 (open item correctly presented).** Whether goose's default version table has a unique `version_id` is stated as "unverified here — I am not asserting it either way" (`migration-approach.md:40`), with the actionable backstop independent of the answer; `decisions/migration-approach-objections.md:25-27` records why it was left open; `containerization-design.md:80` repeats "regardless of whether goose's default schema already has one". Nowhere is it asserted as fact. Confirmed and left open, as instructed.

**F31 (minor — stale research text).** `research-cockroachdb-postgres-semantics.md:40` still mandates "by SQLSTATE code only — never by matching … constraint-name substrings" and `:55` says "`Update()` in our contract *is* a portable upsert"; both were superseded (`multi-db-strategy.md:298,214`) with no correction marker in the research file, although `05/CLAUDE.md` says corrections are "visible in place". Owner: Priya.

## Seam 8 — `auth_method` and credential storage — CONTRADICTION

**F32 (agree).** `auth_method` as a lookup table with `requires_secret` and `is_active` is consistent across `threat-model.md:8`, `multi-db-strategy.md:43,141-147,382-390`, `api-connector-design-rationale.md:58`, and `migration-approach.md:54-58`; 02's method-aware hashing decision (`threat-model.md:14-17`) targets the reserved columns 05 kept unchanged (`05/PLAN.md:142`); passkey rows carry `secret NULL` in both (`threat-model.md:17` ↔ `multi-db-strategy.md:157,372-376`).

**F33 (contradiction — 01 claims more than the schema supports).** `industry-framing.md:36`: "`webauthn` or `passkey` tomorrow, **without changing the table shape**." `api-connector-design-rationale.md:13`: "a future `"webauthn"` row would have a public key and credential ID instead, not a password field left null by convention." The schema has no public-key, credential-id or counter column; `secret` is "Salted hash only" (`multi-db-strategy.md:363`); and 02 says public-key material "does not belong in `secret`/`hash_algo`/`hash_cost` at all if/when a passkey method is actually added" (`threat-model.md:17`), with counter and credential-id integrity as separate requirements (`:32`). A passkey row today is exactly "a password field left null by convention" (`secret_state = 'none'`), and adding passkeys changes the table shape. Owner: Naomi. Fix, one document (`industry-framing.md:36` and the mirror at `api-connector-design-rationale.md:13`): "without changing the `method` discriminator — method-specific columns or a side table arrive by migration".

**F34 (minor contradiction — column type).** `threat-model.md:12` describes `secret` as "nullable TEXT" and `:15` stores the PHC string "inside the `secret` column's own text"; the contract types it `BYTEA`/`BLOB`, Go `[]byte` (`multi-db-strategy.md:133,363`). Storable, but the description is wrong and `secret_state` (`:134`) is never mentioned by 02. Owner: Marcus. Fix: `threat-model.md:12` → "bytes holding the PHC-encoded string". Latent, no fix now: the table-level CHECK (`:372-376`) requires `hash_algo`/`hash_cost` whenever a secret is set, so an encrypted-not-hashed TOTP secret (`threat-model.md:32`) is unrepresentable — consistent with 02's own statement that TOTP is uncommitted (`handoff-04-secrets.md:22`), but worth one sentence in either document.

1	# Local Dev-Loop
2	
3	Design-only — a docker-compose sketch, not a committed `docker-compose.yml`. Local counterpart to the cluster target in `./containerization-design.md`, not a replacement for it: this is what a developer runs on a laptop; the cluster design is what the submission targets as the real deployment shape.
4	
5	## Two compose profiles, matching 03's `DB_DRIVER` env var
6	
7	```yaml
8	# docker-compose.yml (sketch)
9	services:
10	  api-service:
11	    build: {context: ., dockerfile: cmd/api-service/Dockerfile}
12	    environment:
13	      DB_DRIVER: ${DB_DRIVER:-sqlite}
14	      DB_DSN: ${DB_DSN:-file:/data/dev.db}
15	      HTTP_ADDR: ":8080"
16	    ports: ["8080:8080"]
17	    volumes: ["sqlite-data:/data"]
18	    profiles: ["sqlite"]
19	
20	  api-service-pg:
21	    extends: {service: api-service}
22	    environment:
23	      DB_DRIVER: postgres
24	      DB_DSN: postgres://dev:«REDACTED»@postgres:5432/loginid_dev?sslmode=disable
25	    depends_on: [postgres]
26	    profiles: ["postgres"]
27	
28	  postgres:
29	    image: postgres:16-alpine
30	    environment: {POSTGRES_USER: dev, POSTGRES_PASSWORD: dev, POSTGRES_DB: loginid_dev}
31	    ports: ["5432:5432"]
32	    profiles: ["postgres"]
33	
34	  idp-connector:
35	    build: {context: ., dockerfile: cmd/idp-connector/Dockerfile}
36	    environment:
37	      IDP_ABC_BASE_URL: ${IDP_ABC_BASE_URL:-http://mock-idp:9090}
38	      IDP_ABC_CLIENT_ID: dev-client
39	      IDP_ABC_CLIENT_SECRET: dev-secret
40	    profiles: ["sqlite", "postgres"]
41	
42	volumes:
43	  sqlite-data:
44	```
45	
46	`docker compose --profile sqlite up` for the zero-dependency path (no Postgres container, fastest inner loop); `docker compose --profile postgres up` when a developer needs to exercise the Postgres-specific code path (the `pg_trgm` index, per `../05-data-ops/multi-db-strategy.md`). CockroachDB isn't in the local loop — it's wire-compatible enough with Postgres per 05's strategy doc that the Postgres profile exercises the shared code path; a CockroachDB-specific compose profile would test the same code again, not new code.
47	
48	## Dev credentials are dev credentials
49	
50	The `dev`/`dev` Postgres password and the hardcoded `dev-client`/`dev-secret` above are local-only, never used outside a developer's own machine, and never the pattern used in `./containerization-design.md` or `./secrets-delivery.md` — those go through the mechanisms 02 specifies. Worth stating explicitly so nobody reads this file as the security design.
51	
52	## Migrations locally
53	
54	`goose up` (or whatever 05 finalizes) run by hand or via a `make migrate` target against whichever profile is active — no orchestration-layer exclusivity needed locally since there's exactly one developer and one instance. The R2 requirement (`../05-data-ops/migration-approach.md`) is a production/CI concern; the local loop doesn't need to simulate it.
55	
56	## Hot-reload for `api-service`, dev-profile only
57	
58	Considered cutting `air` as unneeded polish, reconsidered: `docker compose build && up` on every change is a slow inner loop for a project a developer iterates on repeatedly, and the cost of adding it is close to zero — one `air`-based dev target in the compose file, no production image impact since it only applies to the `sqlite`/`postgres` dev profiles, never `./containerization-design.md`'s images. Worth doing, not worth debating further.
59	
60	## What's deliberately not here
61	
62	No local Kubernetes (`kind`/`minikube`) mirroring the lab cluster — compose is the right altitude for a two-service Go project's local loop; a local k8s cluster would be solving a problem this project doesn't have.
63	
64	---
65	*AI tooling note: drafted directly by Theo (Sonnet, this session) from 03's config surface and the containerization design above. Bree's (capability-case) and Callum's (scope-cut) per-deliverable notes reviewed and folded in inline above, per PLAN.md §3.*
66

9	3. **Test.** `go test ./... -race` — unit tests against 03's `internal/dao` per-backend implementations: SQLite in-process for the fast path, plus **two** testcontainer jobs against the shared `postgres` package — one on Postgres, one on CockroachDB. Considered running CockroachDB only in the local dev-loop (wire-compatible enough with Postgres that testing both looked redundant) and reconsidered for CI specifically: wire-compatible isn't schema-compatible, and a Postgres-only test matrix means a CockroachDB-specific SQL quirk (UUID defaults, upsert syntax, lock semantics) surfaces at deploy time instead of at PR time. CockroachDB ships an official single-node Docker image, so this is a config addition to the test job, not new infrastructure — a few extra minutes of CI wall-clock for catching it at the cheapest possible point. `-race` is not optional given two DB implementations and a migration-runner exclusivity requirement (05's R2) that's exactly the kind of thing a race detector catches in test even though it's enforced by orchestration in production.

1	# Observability + Never-Log Enforcement
2	
3	Design-only. Covers logging, metrics, and tracing for both services, and — the harder half — how `handoff-04-secrets.md`'s never-log list gets enforced as a mechanism, not just stated as a policy.
4	
5	## Logging
6	
7	Structured logging (`slog`, Go stdlib — no third-party logging library needed for two services this size), JSON output, one line per event. Every log line that touches a request carries `sub` (caller identity), the endpoint, status, and a request id — never the request or response body wholesale. This is the pattern the never-log list requires (hand-off item 8: "full request bodies of `POST /auth` and `POST /identity`... both contain items above by construction") — the enforcement mechanism is that handlers log a small, explicit field set assembled by hand, never `log.Printf("%+v", req)` or equivalent reflection-based dumps of a request/response struct. A struct that contains a password field can't leak through a log statement that never receives the struct.
8	
9	**Enforcement beyond "write it carefully":**
10	- A lint rule (a `golangci-lint` custom check, or a simple `go vet`-style grep in CI) that flags any log call passing a whole request/response struct, a `*http.Request` body, or any variable named/typed to match the never-log list's fields (`password`, `token`, `secret`, `dsn`, `client_secret`). Not foolproof — a determined developer can rename around it — but it catches the accidental case, which is the common one.
11	- Error-path discipline: wrapped errors are logged with `err.Error()` truncated/sanitized where the error type is known to carry sensitive detail (a DB driver error can echo the DSN; a vendor HTTP client error can echo the response body per hand-off item 4) — the wrapping layer strips those before the error reaches a log call, not the log call itself deciding case-by-case.
12	
13	## Metrics
14	
15	Standard RED metrics (rate, errors, duration) per endpoint, via Prometheus client library, scraped by whatever the lab cluster already runs (not stood up new for this take-home). Labels: method, route, status class — never a label with cardinality tied to user data (no `sub` or phone number as a metric label; that turns a metrics backend into an accidental PII store with no access control at all, which is a worse leak than a log line since metrics systems are typically more widely readable).
16	
17	## Tracing
18	
19	Not built out here — a two-service take-home with no running collector doesn't get more credible by naming OpenTelemetry. If ever added, it follows the same field discipline as logging.
20	
21	## The never-log list, operationalized
22	
23	Hand-off's nine items map to two enforcement surfaces, not nine separate mechanisms:
24	
25	1. **Application-level:** the structured-logging discipline above (explicit field sets, the lint check, error-wrapping sanitization) covers items 1, 2, 5, 6, 7 (password, tokens, JWTs, client secrets, DSN) — these never enter a Go `log`/`slog` call in the first place.
26	2. **Proxy/ingress-level:** item 9 ("search query parameters containing a name or phone fragment... redact or hash before any access log, including proxy and ingress logs 04 controls") is the one item application code can't fully own, since ingress access logs are written by the ingress controller, not the service. Mechanism: an ingress-level log-format override that logs the request path with query string stripped (or a regex redaction on the `phone`/`name` query params specifically) rather than the default full-URL access log format. This is a one-time ingress ConfigMap change, not a per-request code path.
27	3. Item 3 (PII field values from `/identity` or `user_profile`) and item 4 (raw vendor error bodies) are the same discipline as #1 applied to the connector specifically — the connector logs "called vendor X, got status Y," never the response body.
28	
29	## Audit-log access is a separate grant from Secret access
30	
31	Per the hand-off's Kubernetes-specific requirements: the log stream carrying `sub`/scope/record-id/timestamp (the "fact of access" the never-log list still permits) is itself sensitive enough to reconstruct who looked up whom, so it gets its own RBAC — a distinct, narrower `Role` bound to an incident-review group, separate from any ServiceAccount's `Secret`-read grant (`./secrets-delivery.md`). Mechanism: if logs go to a cluster-local sink (e.g., Loki), that sink's read API gets its own RBAC/auth in front of it, not the same one guarding `kubectl get secrets`; if logs go to an external platform, the equivalent is a scoped read-only role in that platform, provisioned separately from any cluster credential.
32	
33	## Retention-sweep observability (05's S5 requirement, final)
34	
35	Per `../05-data-ops/pii-governance.md`: the retention sweep must emit, per data class per run, three metrics — rows examined, rows deleted, and **the age of the oldest surviving row in that class**. An alert fires when that age exceeds the class's stated retention window plus one sweep interval. This is the one metric in this document tied to a specific reasoning worth repeating: a sweep job that silently stops running produces no error and no symptom on its own — "rows deleted" can't tell a broken sweep from a legitimately empty one, since both report zero. Oldest-surviving-row age is the one number that keeps climbing when the job is dead and holds steady when it's healthy, which is why it's the alerting signal, not just a nice-to-have metric alongside the other two.
36	
37	Mechanism: the sweep job (whatever invokes it — a CronJob against the lab cluster, per 05's mechanism-agnostic requirement) emits these as Prometheus metrics with a `class` label (one label value per retention class, e.g. `vendor_token_cache`, `idp_identity_cache` — never a label carrying the PII itself, same discipline as the RED-metrics rule above) rather than only a log line, since a log line nobody's alerting on has the same "silent failure" problem the requirement exists to solve. The alert rule is `oldest_surviving_row_age_seconds{class="X"} > (retention_window_seconds + sweep_interval_seconds)`, evaluated per class.
38	
39	## What's deliberately not here
40	
41	No SLOs/error-budget policy, no alerting-rule catalog, no log-retention-window design (that's 05's PII-retention territory for anything log-adjacent to PII, per `../05-data-ops/pii-governance.md` — this document only says logs must exclude PII values in the first place, not how long the remaining metadata is kept). A take-home needs the shape of an observability approach and a credible enforcement mechanism for the one hard requirement (never-log); it doesn't need a full SRE runbook.
42	
43	---
44	*AI tooling note: drafted directly by Theo (Sonnet, this session) from `handoff-04-secrets.md` v2's never-log list. Bree's (capability-case) and Callum's (scope-cut) per-deliverable notes reviewed and folded in inline above, per PLAN.md §3.*

What's left (chiefly the audit trail: `sub`, scope, opaque record id, timestamp — permitted by design, pseudonymous rather than PII-free) still needs a retention window, and that window's enforcement site is **this track's**: the log sink's own retention policy (the Loki/cluster-local sink named under \"Audit-log access,\" above, or the external platform's retention setting if logs go there instead), configured to 05's audit-log retention row in `pii-governance.md`'s table. Not a deletion job 04 writes — a retention setting on infrastructure 04 already operates.\n\n## What's deliberately not here\n\nNo SLOs/error-budget policy, no alerting-rule catalog beyond the retention-sweep alert above. A take-home needs the shape of an observability approach and a credible enforcement mechanism for the one hard requirement (never-log); it doesn't need a full SRE runbook."

Another Claude session sent a message: <cross-session-message from="uds:/tmp/cc-socks/36556.sock" from-name="data-ops-planning-phase" from-mode="prompting"> **F28 from the consistency pass — `migration-approach.md` §2 has changed, and your CI migration stage cites it.** Your design does not change; the directory contents do. **What was wrong.** §2 put every `CREATE TABLE` in `shared/`, with `postgres/` holding one index file. That cannot work: the field-level schema in `multi-db-strategy.md` §5 gives **every** table per-engine DDL — `UUID DEFAULT gen_random_uuid()` vs `TEXT`, `TEXT COLLATE "C"` vs `TEXT COLLATE BINARY`, a regex `CHECK` vs a `GLOB` `CHECK`, `TIMESTAMPTZ` vs `TEXT`, `BYTEA` vs `BLOB`, `BOOLEAN` vs `INTEGER`. SQLite's type affinity would have quietly absorbed the differing *type names*, which is exactly why the claim looked survivable, but it does nothing for collation clauses or CHECK syntax — those are hard syntax differences. **The corrected layout — these are the changed lines:** ``` migrations/ shared/ # seed data only — no CREATE TABLE postgres/ # all DDL for Postgres + CockroachDB, plus the trigram index sqlite/ # all DDL for SQLite ``` - `shared/` — the `auth_method` seed row and nothing else. - `postgres/` — full DDL for `auth_method`, `user_profile`, `user_credential`, `deletion_log` in Postgres/CockroachDB types, plus the `pg_trgm` expression index on `LOWER(name)`. - `sqlite/` — the same four tables in SQLite types and collations, no trigram index. **Nothing in R1–R5 changes, and neither does your mechanism.** Two invocations, separate version tables, single-runner exclusion from your orchestration layer, fail-closed on both version tables, two credentials, rollback-not-a-recovery-plan — all unchanged. The document promised this outcome as a fallback and I am now promoting it, not inventing it. **Two things that read *better* after the change**, worth a moment since they touch your stage: Your R3 fail-closed check now has a more obvious justification. The worked example — `shared/` current while the `pg_trgm` index is unapplied — was slightly strained when `shared/` held the tables; now that `shared/` holds only seed data and all DDL is per-backend, "both version tables must be checked" is plainly necessary rather than defensive. And the `CREATE EXTENSION`/`pg_trgm` provisioning you broke out into its own credential row stays exactly right — the extension and its index are still Postgres-only, still absent on CockroachDB, still nonexistent on SQLite. **How this happened, since it bears on your trust in my documents.** The correct answer was already written in §2 as a hypothetical fallback, contingent on an open item marked "under review in story S1". S1 closed and resolved it in favour of that fallback, and I never updated this document — so a file marked "final" carried a provisional default its own dependency had already overruled. The layout error is the smaller half; the real failure is that I marked a document final while an item inside it was still pointing at an open question. No open items remain in it now. </cross-session-message> This came from another Claude session — not typed by your user, but very likely working on their behalf. 33	## Retention-sweep observability (05's S5 requirement, final)
34	
35	Per `../05-data-ops/pii-governance.md`: the retention sweep must emit, per data class per run, three metrics — rows examined, rows deleted, and **the age of the oldest surviving row in that class**. An alert fires when that age exceeds the class's stated retention window plus one sweep interval. This is the one metric in this document tied to a specific reasoning worth repeating: a sweep job that silently stops running produces no error and no symptom on its own — "rows deleted" can't tell a broken sweep from a legitimately empty one, since both report zero. Oldest-surviving-row age is the one number that keeps climbing when the job is dead and holds steady when it's healthy, which is why it's the alerting signal, not just a nice-to-have metric alongside the other two.
36	
37	Mechanism: the sweep job (whatever invokes it — a CronJob against the lab cluster, per 05's mechanism-agnostic requirement) emits these as Prometheus metrics with a `class` label matching 05's actual retention classes — `source="direct"`, `source="idp_cache"`, and `orphan` for an orphaned `idp_cache` row (`../05-data-ops/pii-governance.md`), never a label carrying the PII itself, same discipline as the RED-metrics rule above. (An earlier draft of this section invented `vendor_token_cache`/`idp_identity_cache` as class names — those don't correspond to anything 05 tracks and described a connector token cache the S3 ruling never adopted; corrected here.) The alert rule is `oldest_surviving_row_age_seconds{class="X"} > (retention_window_seconds + sweep_interval_seconds)`, evaluated per class.
38

  "new_string": "Mechanism: the sweep job (whatever invokes it — a CronJob against the lab cluster, per 05's mechanism-agnostic requirement) emits these as Prometheus metrics with a `class` label matching 05's actual retention classes — `source=\"direct\"`, `source=\"idp_cache\"`, and `orphan` for an orphaned `idp_cache` row (`../05-data-ops/pii-governance.md`), never a label carrying the PII itself, same discipline as the RED-metrics rule above. (An earlier draft of this section invented `vendor_token_cache`/`idp_identity_cache` as class names — those don't correspond to anything 05 tracks and described a connector token cache the S3 ruling never adopted; corrected here.) The alert rule is `oldest_surviving_row_age_seconds{class=\"X\"} > (retention_window_seconds + sweep_interval_seconds)`, evaluated per class.\n\n**Batched sweeps: only a drained result feeds the metric.** 05's `DeleteExpired` is batched (a bounded `maxRows` per call, `SweepResult.Drained` marking the last batch of a sweep) — `OldestSurvivingAt` is meaningful only on a `Drained == true` result, since mid-sweep the oldest deletable row is still present by construction and would read as stale on every batch but the last. The sweep job emits `oldest_surviving_row_age_seconds` only from drained results, never from an intermediate batch; the alert rule above already only ever sees what's published, so this is a constraint on what the job emits, not an extra filter the alert needs to apply. Getting this wrong means the alert fires on every sweep and gets muted, which is worse than not having it."
}

The only way to keep the verification code path (every `api-service` replica) from being able to read the private key is to never mount it there at all. `APP_MODE=issuer` on the second Deployment is 03's addition to the config surface (env-var only, no flags, per `PLANNING.md`); the same binary handles both modes, wired by that variable, not two separate images. This was F13 in the consistency pass — the earlier version of this manifest mounted the key into `api-service` directly, which the RBAC comment here used to (incorrectly) imply was sufficient.\n\n`idp-connector`'s manifest is the same shape with its own two `Secret` volume mounts (per-vendor client credentials, one `Secret` per vendor per handoff row #5), its own `resources` block (`idp-connector` is I/O-bound waiting on vendor calls, not CPU-heavy — requests `{cpu: 50m, memory: 64Mi}`, limits `{cpu: 250m, memory: 128Mi}`), and no inbound `Service` — it is called by `api-service` or invoked as a job, never exposed to anything outside the cluster.
